# Fact ledger: The most affordable places to buy a home near Myrtle Beach (/buyers/affordable-places-to-live/)

Batch 2026-10-b, page 5. The researcher opened every source below on 2026-10-13. One row per claim. **The writer may use rows marked `verified` only.** The researcher filled columns 1 to 7. The verifier fills 8 and 9.

## Verification summary (2026-10-13)

The verifier did none of the research. Every source was opened again on 2026-10-13: the Zillow CSVs and data pages, the AcclaimWeb deed index, the Census data, OSRM, the 2026 levy sheet, the Treasurer bills, FEMA, SC DES, CMS, SC Housing and the HOME plan.

**Counts:** 98 rows. Verified 93. Wrong 5 (rows 9, 10, 13, 30, 98; each note gives supported wording). Unverifiable 0. Not for the page (research context, or too imprecise): rows 17, 31, 44, 61 and the "no city program found" line in row 98.

**Added check (rows 99 to 105, Green Sea and the scope row, 2026-10-13):** all 7 verified, 0 wrong. Re-run from the sources: both Zillow files, a fresh week-by-week AcclaimWeb pull (30,339 deeds, the same total), the deed pages for 5109/1555 and 5091/1609, Census TIGERweb and the ACS codes, OSRM for all 3 routes and for all 436 beach accesses (North Myrtle Beach 97 is the fastest, by 3 seconds), the 2026 levy sheet, the Treasurer bill for PIN 14708030001 (it recomputes to within a cent), and FEMA on all 65 address points. Limits for the writer: do not write "Rank 1" for Green Sea, since the writer brief does not rank it (1 recorded sale); never publish the $75,000 or the $605,000 deed prices; the Zillow value is for Zillow's area, about the size of the ZIP, not the 40 homes in the Census place; do not publish the ACS ZIP value or the 95 housing units; say "about 40 homes" and "a beach access in North Myrtle Beach". Nichols and Hemingway are towns in Marion and Williamsburg counties (confirmed). Ranking rule (15 recorded sales in 12 months): 11 of the 15 places meet it on verified counts (Loris 36, Aynor 18, Longs 141, Conway 358, Little River 581, Myrtle Beach 1,778, Socastee 368, Carolina Forest 539, Surfside Beach 191, North Myrtle Beach 1,672, Garden City 428); Green Sea (1) does not; Georgetown, Pawleys Island and the Georgetown County part of Murrells Inlet have no verified count (row 31 and the Not verified list; the county's own index at georgetowndeeds.com exists but was not pulled), so they cannot be shown to meet it. The summary above still says 98 rows and 14 places; read it as 105 rows and 15 places.

**What was re-run:**

- Zillow: every value and one-year change in rows 2 to 17 matches the August 2026 CSVs.
- Deeds: all 30,339 deeds were pulled week by week and every exclusion count in row 18 reproduces to within 6. All 6,932 kept deeds were re-opened on AcclaimWeb; price, date and book/page match on every one. The medians, quartiles and condo counts recompute. The 10-a verifiers checked a sample; this check covered all of them.
- Census (data.census.gov access endpoint), OSRM (all 44 routes, to the tenth of a second), the beach pick (60 nearest accesses routed), the 2026 levy sheet, 15 Treasurer bills and FEMA: all match. FEMA was also recounted on every address point (sample figures hold within sampling error), and the three Georgetown County rows reproduce exactly.
- No row states an interest rate or a payment amount. The quote in row 96 carries "no monthly payment"; do not copy it.

**What the writer must know:**

1. **Socastee, Carolina Forest and Garden City are ZIP figures** (rows 9, 10, 13, now wrong as worded). Each Census place is only a sixth to a third of its ZIP. ZIP 29576 is Zillow's "Murrells Inlet" ZIP: the "Garden City" price ($400,213) and the Murrells Inlet price ($400,349) are one area. Use row 16 wording for all ZIP figures. In the deciding-fact table below, read ranks 8, 9 and 12 (Socastee, Carolina Forest, Garden City) as ZIPs.
2. **Longs is a 2-mile circle** in the deed, tax and flood rows, Zillow's own area in the price row, and no Census place. Say "within 2 miles of the Longs town point" for deeds, tax and flood.
3. **The ranking is of the 14 places checked.** Hemingway, Nichols and Green Sea (row 17) are cheaper than Georgetown. Say "lowest of the places checked". Little River and Myrtle Beach are within 0.2 percent; Socastee's ZIP is within 0.1 percent.
4. **Deed prices are "recorded sales", not "resales".** The county cannot flag non-arm's-length sales above $10,000. About 50 kept deeds cover several parcels or tracts. Sixteen sellers with 5 or more units are not in the builder list (Little River's middle is $265,000 without them, not $283,000). Loris (36) and Aynor (18) are small samples. Condo-style counts are minimums.
5. **Row 30 should not say the sources "agree".** The percentages are right; the comparison sets different areas and measures side by side, and the Surfside Beach explanation is not shown.
6. **Drive times** are free-flow, from one town point, and the mall is a shopping stop, not a work centre. Seven of the 14 nearest beach accesses are listed with no car parking (Loris, Longs, Aynor, Little River, Carolina Forest, Garden City) or golf cart parking only (Socastee); the nearest access with parking adds at most 1.5 minutes. Carolina Forest is measured from one park parcel. The hospital list may be incomplete (McLeod Health Carolina Forest was not checked), and CMS shows McLeod Loris with no emergency services while McLeod says it has emergency care: write "hospital", not "emergency room".
7. **Taxes** are for a home that receives the 4 percent legal-residence assessment; the owner must receive it (S.C. Code 12-43-220(c), 12-37-220(B)(47)). Each place is one sample bill. Georgetown's $909 comes from a calculator with no tax year and is not comparable with the 2026 Horry bills.
8. **Flood shares** are of address points (all buildings), not homes. Georgetown County rows use a different method (Census block centers) and are not comparable. Zone X is minimal hazard, not none.
9. **ACS values** are owner-reported, pooled over 2020 to 2024, with margins. Do not publish Pawleys Island (margin 37 percent) or Aynor's year built (margin 13 years). Do not set an ACS value beside a Zillow value as if they measured the same thing.
10. **Zillow's own percentile range is inconsistent** (35th to 65th on the data page, "middle third" in the methodology). Do not state one. September data is due about mid-October: re-download and change every figure together.
11. **SC Housing:** row 98 left out the buyer subsidy in the HOME plan. County First pages call counties "underserved" and "distressed"; do not repeat that about Horry. The live /buyers/programs/ page does state "Interest Rate 0%" and "Monthly Payments None" (confirmed on the live-branch clone), against the CLAUDE.md rule; that is for the site fix list, not this page.

**Added 2026-10-13 (Green Sea, rows 99 to 105).** Green Sea ($201,215, up 5.9 percent, row 99) is below every place in the table, so it is rank 1 of 15 places checked and every rank above moves down one. Green Sea is a Census designated place, not a town. Nichols and Hemingway are cheaper on Zillow but the towns are outside Horry and Georgetown counties and are left out on purpose (row 105).

## Method notes for the verifier

- **Scratch scripts.** Python, run 2026-10-13. Every query URL is in its row. The deed pull returned 30,339 DEED (001) rows for 10/1/2025 to 9/30/2026, the same total as the 2026-10-a ledgers.
- **Owner names.** The Treasurer bills and deed index show private owner names. This ledger cites PINs and county legal descriptions only.
- **Town points.** USGS GNIS populated-place points (the traditional town center), except Carolina Forest (no GNIS place point; a county park parcel on Carolina Forest Boulevard). These are not addresses.
- **Three geographies.** Zillow "city" areas are Zillow's; Census places are Census boundaries; tax districts are the county's. They overlap but are not the same set. Each row says which one it uses.
- **Never record:** interest rates or payment amounts (the SC Housing documents state the lien rate and payment terms; left out on purpose), who lives anywhere, crime, schools by rating. ACS income was pulled but not recorded, because it describes people, not homes.

Residential = county LandUseCode starting with 1 and MarketImprv above 0 (the county does not publish code meanings). Location = the county address point for the parcel, or the parcel's polygon center when there is none. Builder = grantor name of a home builder or lot seller (DR HORTON, LENNAR, NVR, PULTE, MERITAGE, CLAYTON PROPERTIES, GREAT SOUTHERN HOMES, BEVERLY HOMES, STARLIGHT HOMES, BEAZER, ASHTON CHARLESTON, TOLL SOUTHEAST and similar; the full list is in affordable-sales.json notes). Tax sale = 'DELINQUENT TAX', 'SHERIFF' or 'MASTER IN EQUITY' in a party name; corrective = 'CORRECT' or 'RE-RECORD' in the memo. Every kept sale, with book/page, PIN and price, is in batches/2026-10-b/data/affordable-sales.json. Quartiles: inclusive method. | 2026-10-13 (index through 9/30/2026) | 2026-11 | verified | The counts reproduce. I pulled DEED (001) from AcclaimWeb week by week: 30,339 deeds; 287 have no parcel number (190 of them under $10,000, so the 10,983 is counted after the 287); 113 parcels are not in the county layer; 5,710 are not residential with a building (row says 5,709); 13,194 remain (row says 13,188; my tax-sale and corrective pattern found 52 deeds, the row's found 59). Builder sales: 822 (sum of the place rows). Every kept parcel has a 1xx land-use code and a building value. Limits the writer must keep. (1) Zero-price and under-$10,000 deeds are out (11,075 deeds had no price). Non-arm's-length sales above $10,000 (relatives, related companies) cannot be told apart; the county does not flag them (same limit as 2026-10-a row 18(b)). Test: dropping sales priced under 40 percent or over 2.5 times the county's own market value moves each median by 2.5 percent or less. (2) The 'one parcel only' filter catches only an empty parcel field. About 50 kept deeds list one parcel but the memo says several parcels, lots or tracts (for example '3 PARCELS' $420,000 in Conway; '2 TRACTS' $30,000 in Loris and $25,000 in Conway, the lowest price in each; 'SEVERAL PARCELS' $14,775,000 in Longs and $1,100,000 in Little River). Without them each median moves by $3,500 or less. (3) 'Builder' is by seller name. Sixteen other sellers each sold 5 or more units in the year (118 deeds in Myrtle Beach, 56 in Little River, 28 in Carolina Forest, 13 in Socastee, 10 in Surfside Beach, 8 in North Myrtle Beach, 7 in Conway). They look like developers or bulk investors. Without them Little River's middle is $265,000 (not $283,000), Myrtle Beach $234,950, Carolina Forest $350,000. So write 'recorded sales not from a listed builder', never 'resales' or 'homes resold'. Count is deeds, not homes (99 parcels appear twice). Dates are recording dates; the price is the clerk-typed consideration. Safe wording: From October 2025 through September 2026, [place] had N recorded sales of homes, not from a listed builder, with a middle price of $X [Horry County Register of Deeds, recorded deeds on residential parcels]. |

| 30 | (added 2026-10-13) The two price sources agree within about 15 percent for Loris, Longs, Aynor, Conway, Little River, Socastee, Carolina Forest and North Myrtle Beach. They disagree a lot for Myrtle Beach (deed middle $227,750 against Zillow $323,058, -30%), Surfside Beach (deed middle $530,000 against Zillow $362,259, +46%), Garden City (deed middle $306,500 against Zillow $400,213, -23%). In Myrtle Beach and Garden City most recorded resales were condominium-style units, which pull the sale middle down; in Surfside Beach most were not, which pulls it up. Zillow's index is a value for all homes, not a middle of sales. Socastee, Carolina Forest and Garden City are compared with a ZIP code that is not the same area. | See claim | Calculation from the ZHVI rows and the sale-price rows | Rows above | Loris: Zillow 255,122; deeds 237,500 (n=36); deeds / Zillow - 1 = -6.9%; Longs: Zillow 285,812; deeds 275,000 (n=141); deeds / Zillow - 1 = -3.8%; Conway: Zillow 288,359; deeds 259,950 (n=358); deeds / Zillow - 1 = -9.9%; Aynor: Zillow 292,422; deeds 250,650 (n=18); deeds / Zillow - 1 = -14.3%; Little River: Zillow 322,487; deeds 283,000 (n=581); deeds / Zillow - 1 = -12.2%; Myrtle Beach: Zillow 323,058; deeds 227,750 (n=1778); deeds / Zillow - 1 = -29.5%; Socastee: Zillow 323,494; deeds 280,000 (n=368); deeds / Zillow - 1 = -13.4%; Carolina Forest: Zillow 358,049; deeds 356,300 (n=539); deeds / Zillow - 1 = -0.5%; Surfside Beach: Zillow 362,259; deeds 530,000 (n=191); deeds / Zillow - 1 = +46.3%; North Myrtle Beach: Zillow 395,960; deeds 375,000 (n=1672); deeds / Zillow - 1 = -5.3%; Garden City: Zillow 400,213; deeds 306,500 (n=428); deeds / Zillow - 1 = -23.4% | 2026-10-13 | 2026-11 | wrong | The arithmetic recomputes (every percentage matches). The wording does not hold. 'Agree within about 15 percent' sets a deed middle against a Zillow figure for a different area in four of the eight places: Longs (2-mile circle against Zillow's own area), Socastee and Carolina Forest (Census CDP against ZIP), Little River (Census CDP against Zillow's area). Aynor's -14.3 percent rests on 18 sales, and Little River falls to about -18 percent without the 56 bulk-seller deeds (row 23). The Surfside Beach cause ('most were not condos, which pulls it up') is not shown: non-condo sales alone have a middle of $590,000, far above Zillow's $362,259, so the mix does not explain it. The Myrtle Beach and Garden City condo explanation is supported (condo middle $165,000 and $222,500). Supported wording: Zillow's typical value and the middle of recorded sales are different measures and are not expected to match. In Carolina Forest, Conway, Loris and Longs the two are within about 10 percent; in Myrtle Beach the recorded middle is 30 percent lower and in Surfside Beach 46 percent higher [Zillow Research ZHVI; Horry County Register of Deeds]. Show the numbers; do not say the sources 'agree'. |

So a home you live in pays tax on $4,000 for every $100,000 of county appraised value, at the levy less 109.1 mills. | 4% ratio; school operating 109.1 exempt | S.C. Code 12-43-220(c) and 12-37-220(B)(47)(a), as verified in Del Webb Grande Dunes rows 70 and 71 (2026-10-a) | https://www.scstatehouse.gov/code/t12c043.php ; https://www.scstatehouse.gov/code/t12c037.php | 12-37-220(B)(47)(a): "is exempt from all property taxes imposed for school operating purposes but not including millage imposed for the repayment of general obligation debt." Every 4 percent bill below shows a "Residential Exemption" equal to assessed value x 0.1091. | 2026-10-13 | 2027-01 | verified | Both statutes re-opened on scstatehouse.gov. 12-43-220(c)(1): the legal residence and up to five acres, owned and occupied by the owner, is assessed at 4 percent. 12-37-220(B)(47)(a): owner-occupied residential property 'eligible for and receiving' that ratio is exempt from school operating taxes, not debt millage. Completeness: the owner must receive the 4 percent ratio (it is not automatic); a second home or rental is assessed at 6 percent and pays the school operating levy. Safe wording: A home that is the owner's legal residence and receives the 4 percent assessment pays no school operating tax, but still pays school debt tax [S.C. | 77 | (added 2026-10-13) (Method row for the flood rows.) FEMA's high-risk flood areas are the Special Flood Hazard Area (zones A, AE, AO, VE; field SFHA_TF = "T"). For each Horry County place, 400 home locations were drawn at random (seed 42) from the county's address points inside the Census boundary (one per location, duplicates at the same spot removed) and tested against FEMA's National Flood Hazard Layer. For Georgetown, Pawleys Island and Murrells Inlet, whose address points are not in the Horry layer, every 2020 Census block with homes inside the boundary was tested at its center and weighted by its 2020 housing units. | Method | FEMA National Flood Hazard Layer, layer 28 (Flood Hazard Zones); Horry County address points (layer 22); Census TIGERweb places and 2020 blocks (HU100) | https://hazards.fema.gov/arcgis/rest/services/public/NFHL/MapServer/28/query ; https://www.horrycounty.org/parcelapp/rest/services/HorryCountyGISApp/MapServer/22/query ; https://tigerweb.geo.census.gov/arcgis/rest/services/TIGERweb/Tracts_Blocks/MapServer/2/query | FEMA query: geometryType esriGeometryMultipoint (the sample), where SFHA_TF='T', outFields FLD_ZONE, DFIRM_ID; each sample point tested against the returned polygons. Panels: DFIRM 45051C (Horry), 45043C (Georgetown). | 2026-10-13 | 2027-10 | verified | Method reproduced, but with a full count in place of a 400-point sample: the seed-42 draw cannot be rebuilt from the description. Horry address points and NFHL layer 28 re-queried. Group: the Horry address layer holds every address (houses, condos, businesses), not only homes, and a condo building counts once, so call the unit 'address points', not 'homes'. The Georgetown County rows use a different method (2020 housing units by Census block center), so they are not comparable with the Horry rows. A FEMA high-risk zone is not the same as flood risk: zone X means minimal hazard, not none. |

| 107 | (added 2026-10-13, writer, pending verification (round 2)) When a home sells, South Carolina treats the deed as an "assessable transfer of interest": the county values the home at its fair market value as of December 31 of the year of the sale, and the 15 percent limit on value increases does not apply to that value. So a buyer's tax is figured on about what the home is worth in the year it is bought. Page wording: "When you buy, the county values the home at about what it is worth that year." | Rule | S.C. Code 12-37-3150(A)(1) and 12-37-3140(A)(1)(b) and (B) | https://www.scstatehouse.gov/code/t12c037.php | 12-37-3150(A): "an assessable transfer of interest in real property includes, but is not limited to, the following: (1) a conveyance by deed". 12-37-3140(A)(1): "the fair market value of real property is its fair market value applicable for the later of: ... (b) December thirty-first of the year in which an assessable transfer of interest has occurred". 12-37-3140(B): "this limit does not apply ... to the fair market value of real property when an assessable transfer of interest occurred in the year that the transfer value is first subject to tax." | Read 2026-10-13 | 2027-07 | verified with corrected wording | Re-opened https://www.scstatehouse.gov/code/t12c037.php on 2026-10-13 (2026-10-13). 12-37-3150(A)(1) lists "a conveyance by deed" as an assessable transfer of interest. 12-37-3140(A)(1)(b) sets fair market value "applicable for the later of ... December thirty-first of the year in which an assessable transfer of interest has occurred". 12-37-3140(B) caps countywide-reappraisal increases at 15 percent in five years, then says the limit does not apply "to the fair market value of real property when an assessable transfer of interest occurred in the year that the transfer value is first subject to tax." The quotes in the row are exact. Two limits the row left out. (1) Timing: 12-37-3140(E) says changes in value from a transfer "are first subject to property tax in the following tax year", so a 2026 buyer's new value first appears on the 2027 bill, not the 2026 bill. (2) The statute sets fair market value, not the price paid; it does not say the county uses the sale price. Page sentence "When you buy, the county values the home at about what it is worth that year" is a fair plain reading and may stay. The row's last sentence ("a buyer's tax is figured on about what the home is worth in the year it is bought") is wrong on timing; do not use it. Safe wording: When a home is bought, the county sets its value at fair market value for that year, and the 15 percent limit on increases does not apply to that new value; the new value is first taxed the next year [S.C. Code 12-37-3140(A)(1)(b), (B), (E); 12-37-3150(A)(1)]. Optional page addition: "The new value shows up on the next year's tax bill." 2026-10-13. |

Rounded to the nearest $100: Georgetown about $2,300; Loris about $1,800; Longs about $1,100; Conway about $1,900; Aynor about $1,700; Myrtle Beach about $1,300. Conway with the typical HOA fee on a house ($85 a month, no-hoa ledger row 6): about $2,900 a year. Rests on row 107 (the county values a bought home at about its worth). Leaves out the homestead exemption for owners 65 and older. | See claim | Calculation from rows 2 to 8, 64 to 68, 73, 76, 107; no-hoa-facts.md row 6 | Rows above | Georgetown 251,667 x 0.009088 + 44 = 2,331; Loris 255,122 x 0.006924 = 1,766; Longs 285,812 x 0.003788 + 61.90 = 1,145; Conway 288,359 x 0.006536 = 1,885; Aynor 292,422 x 0.005984 = 1,750; Myrtle Beach 323,058 x 0.00404 = 1,305. Conway 1,885 + 85 x 12 = 2,905. | 2026-10-13 | 2027-07 | verified | Recomputed 2026-10-13 from the cited rows (Zillow Aug 2026 values in rows 2 to 8; mills and fee from rows 64 to 68, 73, 76; ratio 4 percent). Georgetown 251,666.72 x 0.2272 x 0.04 = 2,287.15 + 44 = 2,331 (about $2,300). Loris 255,122.29 x 0.1731 x 0.04 = 1,766 (about $1,800). Longs 285,811.60 x 0.0947 x 0.04 = 1,082.65 + 61.90 = 1,145 (about $1,100; with the district 200 bill, 91.9 mills + $89.40 fee, it is 1,140, the same). Conway 288,359.18 x 0.1634 x 0.04 = 1,885 (about $1,900). Aynor 292,422.40 x 0.1496 x 0.04 = 1,750 (about $1,700; it sits on the 1,750 rounding edge, so $1,700 or $1,800 are both within 3 percent). Myrtle Beach 323,058.02 x 0.101 x 0.04 = 1,305 (about $1,300). Conway plus HOA: 1,885 + 85 x 12 (1,020) = 2,905 (about $2,900). No figure is off by 10 percent; the largest rounding gap is 3.4 percent (Longs, 1,145 to 1,100, which is the page's lower-end number). Credits and fees: the 109.1-mill school exemption is inside each net rate, Myrtle Beach's 44.5-mill Local Option Credit is inside its 101.0 mills, Longs carries the $61.90 fee, and Georgetown carries the $44 fee. The cited bills for Loris, Conway and Aynor show no fee and no credit, so none is missing from this row. Limits for the writer. (a) These use Zillow's typical value as the county's value; the county's appraised value for a given home can differ. (b) Each place is one sample bill's rate. (c) Georgetown's figure is the county estimator with no tax year (row 76), not a 2026 bill; the page says "by Georgetown County's tax estimator", which is right. (d) "Georgetown is highest tax" holds for tax alone ($2,331 against $1,885 for Conway). (e) The $2,900 is tax plus HOA, not the HOA alone; the page sentence "Most homes for sale in Conway have an HOA fee, typically about $85 a month. That adds up to about $2,900 a year" reads as if the fee adds up to $2,900. Change the page to say the tax and the fee together are about $2,900 a year. The $85 is the median fee on Conway detached houses for sale (middle half $63 to $97; no-hoa ledger row 6), across the whole Conway listing search, which includes homes outside the city that pay the lower county tax. 23.3 percent of those listings have no fee, so "most have a fee" holds. 2026-10-13. |

The source supports: a city almost 300 years old; a commercial district on the waterfront with boutique shops and restaurants; preserved 18th and 19th century architecture. It does not say: "street" (it says district); that the shops and restaurants are in the old buildings (the architecture is the city's, not tied to the shops); "downtown" (the word is not on the page); "walkable" is the city's own promotional word for a lifestyle, with no measure behind it. | 116 | (added 2026-10-14, writer, pending verification (round 8)) (Method row for rows 117 to 128.) Every deed in the method row's pull (row 18: 30,339 DEED 001 deeds, 10/1/2025 to 9/30/2026) was matched by parcel number to the county parcel layer. Kept: $10,000 or more; one parcel; no tax-sale, sheriff, master-in-equity or corrective deeds, and no comment naming several parcels, tracts or lots; land use code starting 1 (residential) with a building value above 0; no WEEK, TIMESHARE or INTERVAL legal descriptions; price between 0.4 and 2.5 times the county's market value. 12,812 sales remain. Each sale's neighborhood is the county legal description before the first ';', with PH, LOT, UNIT, BLDG and similar words cut. Condo units (legal description says HPR or UNIT) and land use 107 are left out of the neighborhood prices; land use 120 and 130 (meaning not published by the county) are not ranked. Neighborhoods with 10 or more kept sales were ranked by middle (median) price. The city of Myrtle Beach as a whole is the comparison only (row 24). Page wording: "We joined the county's deed records to its map of lots, so every sale has a neighborhood. We left out condos and sales far below a home's value, and kept neighborhoods where at least 10 homes sold." | Method; 12,812 kept sales | Horry County Register of Deeds (AcclaimWeb deed index); Horry County GIS parcels (layer 24) and address points (layer 22) | https://acclaimweb.horrycounty.org/AcclaimWeb/search/SearchTypeDocType (Doc Type DEED (001), record dates 10/1/2025 to 9/30/2026), matched by ParcelNumber to https://www.horrycounty.org/parcelapp/rest/services/HorryCountyGISApp/MapServer/24/query (PIN, LandUseCode, MarketImprv, MarketProp, TaxDistrict, LegalDescr) | Field names as served: Consideration, ParcelNumber, RecordDate, BookPage, DirectName; LandUseCode, MarketImprv, MarketProp, TaxDistrict, LegalDescr | 2026-10-14 | 2027-01 | verified with corrected wording | 2026-10-14. Recomputed from the writer's saved weekly pulls (30,339 deeds; I did not re-pull the year, but re-opened 44 of the deeds on AcclaimWeb, all matching on price and date) and the saved parcel layer, with my own filter written from this row's text: 12,812 kept sales. The exclusions work as stated (under $10,000, tax-sale/sheriff/master-in-equity names, comments naming corrective, re-record, parcels, tracts or lots, land use not starting 1 or no building value, timeshare words, price outside 0.4 to 2.5 of county value). No kept deed in the twelve places shares a book and page with another, so no multi-parcel deed got through. LIMIT THE PAGE DOES NOT STATE: the building-value filter drops every new home the county has not yet valued. That is 49 of 74 Birchwood sales, 100 of 147 Indigo Grove, 63 of 85 Victory Point, 33 of 53 Glenns Bay and 8 of 25 Coopers Bluff (rows 123, 125, 126). [Corrected in round 8b: the 35 new Lennar townhomes I first counted as Parkview are in a different PARKVIEW; see row 157.] It is not 'sales far below a home's value'. Land use 120 and 130 are skipped (Park at Long Bay about $184,000 and Laurel Woods about $185,000 would otherwise rank first and second) while land use 110 (Golf and Yacht Club) is ranked; the page does not say so. SAFE WORDING: We joined the county's deed records to its map of lots. We left out condos, deeds that cover several lots, sales far below the county's value, and new homes the county has not yet valued, and kept neighborhoods with at least 10 such sales. The page's 'These are the twelve with the lowest prices' is NOT supported: the same method ranks Warner Crossing (229,000), Kingston Oaks (244,000), Village at Sandridge (245,000), Spring Grove (251,000), Andalusia (256,000), Hampton Place (257,500), Oak Forest (257,500, 4 km from Coastal Grand Mall), Forestbrook (260,000), Oak Glenn (261,400), Elmhurst (267,500) and Rivertown Landing (272,000) below the Cove at Glenns Bay (273,500), and none is on the page. Use 'twelve of the lower-priced neighborhoods we checked within about 20 minutes of a public beach access' and say how places were chosen, or add Oak Forest and Forestbrook. |

| 142 | (added 2026-10-14, writer, pending verification (round 8)) Victory Point is a new townhome community off US 501, by Tanger Outlets. It is being built around the old Myrtle Beach Speedway, where cars ran their last laps in 2020. Its street names come from racing: Speedway Street, Victory Way and Talladega Drive. Lennar built most of the townhomes sold here in the past year (row 125). The NAIP 2023 aerial shows the oval; the Victory Point lots sold in 2025 and 2026 are on the southeast part of the old track and the land beside it (county VICTORY POINT PH 2 parcels; corrected 2026-10-14 per the round 10 verifier note on row 180). | Description | WMBF News, 2024-07-10, Makayla Evans | https://www.wmbfnews.com/2024/07/10/old-myrtle-beach-speedway-being-transformed-into-townhome-community ; also https://www.wbtw.com/growthtracker/myrtle-beach-historical-speedway-redeveloping-into-townhome-community/amp/ | "Cars took their last lap around the track in 2020"; the speedway is "right off Highway 501 beside the busy Tanger Outlet intersection." WBTW (search summary): Lennar owns about 14 acres of the former speedway, subdivided into about 175 parcels. Street names: county address points for the 23 sales (Speedway St 19, Victory Way 2, Talladega Dr 2). | 2026-10-14 | 2027-04 | verified with corrected wording | 2026-10-14. Read the WMBF article in full: 'Cars took their last lap around the track in 2020'; 'The land is right off Highway 501 beside the busy Tanger Outlet intersection'; Speedway Villas Holding bought 21 acres in 2022 and plans hundreds of townhomes; no start date. Read the WBTW article in full: the speedway closed in 2020; 'Lennar owns about 14 acres of the former speedway. Speedway Villas Holding LLC owns about 21 acres'; the track area is 'dirt mounds, construction cones and dump trucks'; a resident says houses will go 'on top of where the racetrack once stood'. Neither article names Victory Point, and the '175 parcels' line is not in what I read. Street names Speedway St, Talladega Dr, Victory Way and Pit Crew Dr exist (county roads and address points). CORRECTIONS: 'built around the old Myrtle Beach Speedway' and the caption 'on the land around the track' go beyond the articles, which say the townhomes will replace the track. SAFE WORDING: Victory Point is a new Lennar townhome community off US 501 by Tanger Outlets, on part of the former Myrtle Beach Speedway land, which closed in 2020 [WMBF, WBTW; county parcels]. Several streets have racing names: Speedway Street, Talladega Drive and Pit Crew Drive. ('Victory Way' is not clearly a racing name.) |
| 143 | (added 2026-10-14, writer, pending verification (round 8)) Wild Wing Plantation was once a golf resort with several courses. Three of them closed in 2004. The Avocet course, designed by Jeff Brauer and Larry Nelson, is still open, and some of the Fairways townhomes are near it. The Fairways is inside the city of Conway (tax district 110 on all 20 sales). | Description | Myrtle Beach Golf Trips, Chris King (no date shown); listings for the Fairways (Lot Description "Near Golf Course") | https://www.myrtlebeachgolftrips.com/news/time-to-come-home-why-you-need-to-play-wild-wing/ ; https://mbcoastalhomes.realgeeks.com/property/2622945/ | "once one of Myrtle Beach's most popular multi-course properties"; "prior to the closing of the Woodstork, Falcon and Hummingbird layouts in 2004, leaving only Avocet to carry the mantle"; "a Jeff Brauer-Larry Nelson co-design". Also: "Hummingbird reopened as a 9-hole layout" (so "Three of them closed in 2004" is right; the page does not say all three stayed closed). | 2026-10-14 | 2027-04 | verified with corrected wording | 2026-10-14. Read the myrtlebeachgolftrips.com article in full: 'once one of Myrtle Beach's most popular multi-course properties, prior to the closing of the Woodstork, Falcon and Hummingbird layouts in 2004, leaving only Avocet'; 'a Jeff Brauer-Larry Nelson co-design'; 'Woodstork and Falcon are gone (Hummingbird reopened as a 9-hole layout)'. The article is from about 2020 ('sixteen years later'). A second source (playwildwing.com, read for the neighborhoods ledger) agrees on the Brauer-Nelson design, says Avocet is open and shows a Hummingbird 9-hole course. 'Near it' rests on MLS 'Near Golf Course' in 2 of 5 listings. SAFE WORDING: Three of Wild Wing's courses closed in 2004; one of them later reopened as a nine-hole course. The Avocet course, a Jeff Brauer and Larry Nelson design, is still open. The page text 'Three of them closed in 2004' without the reopening reads as if all three stayed closed; add the reopening or drop the count. |
| 144 | (added 2026-10-14, writer, pending verification (round 8)) Myrtle Beach Golf and Yacht Club is a gated community off Bay Road, from SC 707 near Socastee. A public boat ramp on the Intracoastal Waterway is close by. Staff keep the gate day and night. Inside are a pool, a hot tub, tennis and shuffleboard courts, a playground and a clubhouse. Owners can rent a space for an RV or a trailer. A newer section is Sandpiper Place. | Description | Listing, 6622 Cinnamon Fern Ln (MLS 2524896); MLS Neighborhood field "Sandpiper Place - MB Golf & Yacht" (rows 132) | https://www.tracimiles.com/listings/view/6622-cinnamon-fern-ln-myrtle-beach-sc-real-estate-2524896.html | "the security of a 24/7 staffed entry gate"; "an established, gated community with lots of amenities, including a pool, hot tub, playground, picnic area, pavilion, clubhouse, and tennis and shuffleboard courts"; "the option of a separate storage area on premises for RVs and trailers through the HOA"; "a boat ramp for the Intracoastal Waterway is close by"; "Hwy 707 to Bay Rd. Continue down Bay Rd and make a left turn at Myrtle Beach Golf and Yacht." | 2026-10-14 | 2027-04 | verified with corrected wording | 2026-10-14. Read the Cinnamon Fern listing in full and 16 other listings. GATED: 'Gated' in the MLS amenities of all 12 older-section mbcoastalhomes listings I opened, plus 'locked gate community' and 'gated' text, so gated holds. STAFF: '24/7 staffed entry gate' in one listing (6622 Cinnamon Fern Ln), '24-hour guarded security' in two (6620 Heron Point Blvd and 6616 Royal Pine Dr), '24-hour monitored gate and security patrol' in one (2624979), '24-hour security' in two more (2609551, 2610016). So 'Staff keep the gate day and night' says more than any one source; the sources agree on 24-hour security and a gate. RV and trailer storage: 'option of a separate storage area on premises for RVs and trailers through the HOA' in this listing, with a search summary that says more listings mention it and an extra fee; the page's 'Owners can rent a space' is stronger than the source. Hot tub: this listing and a search summary only. Playground, tennis, shuffleboard, pavilion: several listings. Boat ramp 'close by' and the Bay Road directions confirmed. No HOA or other primary source was found. SAFE WORDING: The community is gated, and listings describe 24-hour security at the gate [listings]. The HOA offers a storage area for RVs and trailers [listing]. Drop 'hot tub' or leave it at one source. |

| 155 | (added 2026-10-14, writer, pending verification (round 8b)) (Method row for round 8b; replaces row 116 for the ranking.) Every deed in row 18's pull (30,339 DEED 001, 10/1/2025 to 9/30/2026) matched by parcel number to the county parcel layer. A deed whose book and page names more than one parcel is left out (several lots). A home is identified by its land use code (starting 1) and its subdivision, not by a building value: a sale with a county building value is kept if its price is 0.4 to 2.5 times the county's value; a sale with no building value yet (a new home the county has not valued) is kept if it is $100,000 or more and either the seller is a builder or the price is at least 2.5 times the county's value of the lot. Sales to a builder are left out (lot sales). The builder list no longer matches the person 'HILDEBRAND ASHTON' (only 'ASHTON WOODS'). Kept: 16,325 sales. Left out: under $10,000 10,983; no building value and likely land (under 2.5 times the lot's value, seller not a builder) 597; comments naming corrective, re-record, several parcels, tracts, lots or open space 548; not residential (land use not starting 1) 486; no building value and under $100,000 466; no parcel number 287; price outside 0.4 to 2.5 times the county value (homes with a building value only) 279; buyer is a builder (a lot sale) 226; parcel not in the layer 98; tax-sale, sheriff, master-in-equity or forfeited-land names 43; timeshare, interval or lease legal descriptions 1. Condos (HPR or UNIT in the legal description, or land use 107) and land use 120 and 130 (manufactured homes on the listings for Laurel Woods and Park at Long Bay) are not in any neighborhood's price. Neighborhood = the legal description before ';' with lot and phase words cut; legal-name variants merged (MB GOLF & YACHT CLUB, M B GOLF & YACHT and MB GOLF & YCHT into MB GOLF & YACHT; COVE AT GLENNS BAY into GLENNS BAY TOWNHOMES); then each name split into places 1.5 km apart (single linkage), because one legal name can cover two places (PARKVIEW, row 157). Rule for the list: at least 10 kept sales, and the drive from the middle of the sales to the nearest public beach access (row 164) rounds to 20 minutes or less (under 22.5). Ranked by middle price. Page wording: "We counted new and resold houses and townhomes. We left out condos, manufactured homes, deeds that cover several lots and sales far below a home's value." "Then we kept the neighborhoods within about 20 minutes' drive of a public beach access, with at least 10 sales." | 16,325 kept sales | Horry County Register of Deeds (AcclaimWeb deed index); Horry County GIS parcels (layer 24) and address points (layer 22); OSRM | Same as row 116 | Script: scratchpad aff-work/r8b/an.py (filters), groups2.py (places), drive.py and final.py (drives) | 2026-10-14 | 2027-01 | verified with corrected wording | 2026-10-14 (round 8b). Rebuilt the filter from this row's text with my own code and my own builder list: 16,329 kept (the row says 16,325; the 4 differ only by builder-name matching). Left-out counts match within a few: under $10,000 10,983; no building value and likely land 599; comments 548; not residential 486; no building value under $100,000 463; no parcel 287; price outside 0.4 to 2.5 times 279; buyer is a builder 223; not in layer 98; tax-sale names 43; timeshare 1. The rule is sound: a new home with no county building value is kept only at $100,000 or more and with a builder seller or a price at least 2.5 times the lot value; lot sales to builders are out; condos, land use 120 and 130 are out; the 'several parcels by book and page' test removes none (the comment test already caught them). Two limits for the writer: the rule keeps a non-builder resale of a not-yet-valued new house (3 in Indigo Grove), which is correct; and 'builder' rests on a name list, so a builder not on it with a price under 2.5 times the lot value would be dropped. My independent grouping, 1.5 km splitting, 10-sale minimum and routed beach times (150 nearest accesses, under 22.5 minutes) give the same list, order and middles as row 156. SAFE WORDING: the page's two sentences are right as worded. |

## Website pages re-checked (2026-10-13)

Pages read on the live branch clone so nothing here contradicts them. Matches need no action; differences are for the owner or a later fix, not this page.

**Match:**

- /buyers/property-taxes/: 109.1 school operating mills; Myrtle Beach $400,000 at 4 percent about $1,615 (16,000 x 101.0 mills = $1,616) and unincorporated about $1,470 (16,000 x 91.9 = $1,470.40); the 53.4 percent city credit; the $89 stormwater fee; the 6 percent to 4 percent ratios for Conway (2.5x), Surfside Beach (3.1x), North Myrtle Beach (3x), Little River (3.3x) and Garden City (3.2x) all recompute from the 2026 levies above.
- /submarkets/carolina-forest/: 2026 levy 201.0 mills (52.1 + 119.1 + fire 21.7 + solid waste 8.1); the 83.4-mill Myrtle Beach levy.
- /submarkets/myrtle-beach/: ACS median owner-occupied value about $388,800 (ACS 2020-2024: 388,800).
- /submarkets/little-river/: Zillow index "near $321,000" (Aug 2026: $322,487).
- /submarkets/surfside-beach/: index "$347,000 to $360,000" (Aug 2026: $362,259, just above) and a blended median around $530,000 (deed resale middle: $530,000).
- /buyers/programs/: Horry is a targeted county; the targeted-county ownership rule; 640 minimum score; 15-year forgiveness; training course; eligible property types. All match the 10.01.2025 handout.
- /buyers/relocating/which-town/: Longs "typically below the beach towns" (Zillow $285,812 against $323,058 for Myrtle Beach and more for the beach towns).

**Differ:**

1. /buyers/programs/ shows "Interest Rate 0%" and "Monthly Payments None" in its assistance box. CLAUDE.md: never state an interest rate or a payment amount. The page does not give the $10,000 amount, the income limits ($114,960 / $134,120) or the $450,000 price limit, and does not mention Palmetto Home Advantage or County First. Its FAQ mentions "county-level options"; none was found (row on the HOME plan).
2. /submarkets/conway/: "Median owner-occupied home value (ACS) about $287,700". ACS 2020-2024 says $260,900 (2019-2023: $236,300; 2018-2022: $202,000). No ACS vintage gives $287,700. Its "medians near $280,000, the lowest entry prices in Horry County" and FAQ "Conway has the lowest entry prices on the Grand Strand": Zillow puts Loris ($255,122) and several rural places (Nichols $192,416, Green Sea $201,215) below Conway ($288,359); ZIP 29527 (Conway) is $261,804.
3. /submarkets/north-myrtle-beach/: ACS "about $452,000". ACS 2020-2024 says $416,900.
4. /submarkets/murrells-inlet/: ACS "about $454,900". ACS 2020-2024 for Murrells Inlet CDP says $366,000; ZCTA 29576 says $340,400.
5. /submarkets/myrtle-beach/: Zillow "near $315,000 in early 2026, down about 3 percent". August 2026: $323,058, up 0.3 percent over August 2025. Likely an older month; name the month if kept.
6. /submarkets/pawleys-island/: Zillow "near $514,000 in mid-2026, up about 5 percent". August 2026 city file: $553,403, up 0.7 percent.
7. /buyers/relocating/which-town/ FAQ: "Off-peak from Myrtle Beach it is about ... 25 to Conway". OSRM gives 24 minutes from Conway to the nearest Myrtle Beach access and 23 to Coastal Grand Mall, so it holds; the other figures there were not re-measured.

## Waiting for MLS

- Median sale price by town and by home type (house, townhome, condo), and days on market. The deed rows cannot split by type except by the county legal description (HPR or UNIT).
- Homes for sale now under a budget (for example under $250,000) in each place, and whether they carry an HOA fee.
- Georgetown, Pawleys Island and the Georgetown County part of Murrells Inlet sale prices.

## Search check (2026-10-13)

Query "most affordable places to live near Myrtle Beach SC". Top results: SoFi's statewide affordable-places list (not the Grand Strand); an AOL/newspaper list of beach towns statewide; the City of Myrtle Beach news item on a USA Today "most desirable" ranking; 2019 news on the metro being "affordable"; Pulte's cost-of-living blog (a builder); 55places' "communities under $200k" (2020). None gives a town-by-town typical value from one source, the drive to the beach and the mall from each town, the tax per $100,000 inside and outside city limits, the flood-zone share, or the state down payment amounts and limits for Horry County. Several quote incomes and rents, which this page will not use.

## Not verified

1. **Georgetown County tax year.** The county calculator (row 76) does not name its tax year. A news report (live5news.com, 2025-10-17) gives a City of Georgetown rate of 129.6 mills after a rollback; a news story is not a source. Tried: gtcountysc.gov Auditor pages (no levy sheet found); Georgetown County GIS hosts gis.gtcounty.org (certificate mismatch), gis.georgetowncountysc.org, gisweb.gtcounty.org and maps.gtcounty.org (connection reset). A Treasurer bill for one Georgetown city parcel would settle it.
2. **Georgetown County sale prices.** Not in the Horry deed index; no Georgetown County deed search was reached.
3. **City or county down payment programs.** No City of Myrtle Beach, City of Conway or Horry County homebuyer down payment program was found (searches of the city sites and the county HOME plan). Not proof that none exists.
4. **Fees on the Surfside Beach ($82.00) and Longs ($61.90) bills.** The bills print "Fees" without naming them. The $89.40 elsewhere is the county stormwater fee (Myrtle Trace row 73).
5. **Which 2.8-mill levy the Longs district pays.** The district total (203.8) is 201.0 plus 2.8; the levy sheet has two 2.8 watershed levies (Buck Creek, Cartwheel). The bill does not itemize.
6. **Zillow "city" boundaries** for unincorporated names (Longs, Little River, Murrells Inlet, Galivants Ferry). Zillow does not publish them with the file.
7. **McLeod Seacoast in CMS.** CMS lists the McLeod license at 3655 Mitchell Street, Loris; the Seacoast campus (4000 Highway 9 East) is not a separate CMS row. Its address comes from McLeod's own page (verified in 2026-10-a, Cypress Village row 49).
8. **Land use code meanings.** The county does not publish them; "residential" here means codes starting with 1 that have a building value.

## Questions only the brokerage can answer

1. In the MLS, what share of homes for sale under $250,000 in Loris, Conway, Longs and Aynor are houses, and what share are condos or manufactured homes? (Replaces the HPR/UNIT split.)
2. Do buyers in the cheaper inland towns usually use SC Housing or Palmetto Home Advantage, and which local lenders are SC Housing partners? (Not a rate question.)

## Summary for the writer

- Rows: 98. All local or federal primary sources (county deed index, county Treasurer and Auditor, Census, FEMA, CMS, USGS, SC DES, SC Housing) except Zillow Research data, which CLAUDE.md allows.
- Added 2026-10-13: rows 99 to 105 (Green Sea, and the Nichols and Hemingway scope row). Green Sea has a Zillow value, drive times, a 2026 tax bill and a flood count, but too few recorded sales for a price (1 sale) and no Census value for the place. Verifier columns for these rows are empty.
- The eight to ten most affordable places by Zillow's index: Georgetown ($251,667), Loris ($255,122), Longs ($285,812), Conway ($288,359), Aynor ($292,422), Little River ($322,487), Myrtle Beach ($323,058), Socastee ($323,494), Carolina Forest ($358,049), Surfside Beach ($362,259).
- What the cheap towns cost in time: Loris about 36 minutes to the nearest beach access and 52 to the mall; Aynor 44 and 42; Conway 24 and 23; Longs 17 and 38; Georgetown 20 and 51.
- What they cost in tax: inside Loris and Conway city limits a home you live in pays $692 and $654 per $100,000, against $368 just outside.
